. Suppose that
p=1
. Let us denote by
M^{\prime}_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
the matrix of cofactors of
M_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
, and by
{}^{t}M^{\prime}_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
its transpose. At least one of the columns of
{}^{t}M^{\prime}_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
is not zero. Since we have that
M_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}.^{t}M^{\prime}_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}=0
, this column determines a non-trivial relation

\tilde{A}\tilde{P}_{i}+\tilde{B}\tilde{Q}_{i}=0

where the coefficients of
\tilde{A},\tilde{B}
are given by the coefficients of this column. Moreover,
\tilde{B}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})=0
since
\tilde{P}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})=0
and
\tilde{Q}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})\neq 0
, and
\tilde{B}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\not\equiv 0
(if not, we would have
\tilde{A}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\equiv 0
since

\tilde{P}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\not\equiv 0
). The coefficients of
\tilde{B}
are homogeneous polynomial formulas in
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}
coefficients of
\tilde{P}_{i}
and
\tilde{d}-1
coefficients of
\tilde{Q}_{i}
. Lifting these formulas to
K[\underline{x},z_{0},\ldots,z_{i-1},z_{i}]
by replacing the
c_{j}
’s by the
z_{j}
’s, we obtain
A
and
B
with
\deg_{\underline{x}}B\leq d_{\underline{x}}\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(\tilde{d}-1),\ \deg_{z_{j}}B\leq d\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\mathbb{\delta}(\tilde{d}-1)
for
j=1,\ldots,i-1
, and
\deg_{z_{i}}B\leq\tilde{d}-1
. We conclude since
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}\leq\mathbb{\delta}
and
\tilde{d}\leq d
.

Suppose that
p\geq 2
. The
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}
columns corresponding to the coefficients of the
{z_{i}}^{k}\tilde{P}_{i}
’s,
k=0,..,\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}-1
, are linearly independent (since they form an upper triangular system). We complete them with
\tilde{d}-p
columns corresponding to the coefficients of the
{z_{i}}^{k}\tilde{Q}_{i}
to a maximal linearly independent family. There is a non-zero minor, say
\Delta
, of maximal order
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\tilde{d}-p
of this family. Proceeding as in Lemma
3.2
, there is a non-zero vector
V
in the kernel of
M_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
whose coefficients are minors of order
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\tilde{d}-p
. More precisely, except for
\Delta
, the other minors are obtained by replacing a column of
\Delta
by the corresponding part of another column of
M_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}
. Hence, they consist of either
\tilde{d}-p+1
columns with coefficients of
\tilde{Q}_{i}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}-1
columns with coefficients of
\tilde{P}_{i}
, or
\tilde{d}-p
columns with coefficients of
\tilde{Q}_{i}
and
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}
columns with coefficients of
\tilde{P}_{i}
. We translate the relation
M_{\tilde{P}_{i},\tilde{Q}_{i}}.V=0
to a non-trivial relation

\tilde{A}\tilde{P}_{i}+\tilde{B}\tilde{Q}_{i}=0

where the coefficients of
\tilde{A},\tilde{B}
are given by the coefficients de
V
. Moreover,

\tilde{B}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})=0
since
\tilde{P}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})=0
and
\tilde{Q}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})\neq 0
, and
\tilde{B}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\not\equiv 0
(if not, we would have
\tilde{A}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\equiv 0
since

\tilde{P}_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},z_{i})\not\equiv 0
). The coefficients of
\tilde{B}
are homogeneous polynomial formulas in at most
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}
coefficients of
\tilde{P}_{i}
and
\tilde{d}-p+1
coefficients of
\tilde{Q}_{i}
. Lifting these formulas to
K[\underline{x},z_{0},\ldots,z_{i-1},z_{i}]
by replacing the
c_{j}
’s by the
z_{j}
’s, since
p\geq 2
, we obtain
A
and
B
with
\deg_{\underline{x}}B\leq d_{\underline{x}}\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(\tilde{d}-1),\ \deg_{z_{j}}B\leq d\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}+\mathbb{\delta}(\tilde{d}-1)
for
j=1,\ldots,i-1
, and
\deg_{z_{i}}B\leq\tilde{d}-1
. We conclude since
\tilde{\mathbb{\delta}}\leq\mathbb{\delta}
and
\tilde{d}\leq d
.
∎

We denote by
B_{1}
the polynomial
B
of the previous lemma. In any case, we are in position to replace
P
by
B_{1}
, with
\deg_{\underline{x}}B_{1}\leq d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(d-1),\ \deg_{z_{j}}B_{1}\leq(2d-1)\mathbb{\delta}
for
j=1,\ldots,i-1
, and
\deg_{z_{i}}B_{1}\leq d-1
. We obtain another Bézout identity:

A_{2}B_{1}+B_{2}Q_{i}=R_{2}

with
R_{2}
the resultant of
B_{1}
and
Q_{i}
with respect to
z_{i}
,

\deg_{\underline{x}}{R_{2}}\leq(d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(d-1))\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(d-1)=d_{\underline{x}}{\mathbb{\delta}}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}((d-1)\mathbb{\delta}+(d-2)+1),

likewise, for
j=1,\ldots,i-1
,

\deg_{z_{j}}{R_{2}}\leq d\mathbb{\delta}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}((d-1)\mathbb{\delta}+(d-2)+1).

Moreover,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\deg_{\underline{x}}{B_{2}}&\leq&(\deg_{\underline{x}}{B_{1}})\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(\deg_{z_{i}}{B_{1}}-1)\\
&\leq&(d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(d-1))\mathbb{\delta}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(d-1-1)=d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(\mathbb{\delta}(d-1)+d-2),\end{array}

and likewise, for
j=1,\ldots,i-1
,

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\deg_{z_{j}}{B_{2}}&\leq&(\deg_{z_{j}}B_{1})\mathbb{\delta}+(\deg_{z_{i}}B_{1}-1)\mathbb{\delta}\\
&\leq&(2d-1)\mathbb{\delta}^{2}+(d-2)\mathbb{\delta}=d\mathbb{\delta}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}(\mathbb{\delta}(d-1)+d-2),\end{array}

and

\deg_{z_{i}}B_{2}\leq\deg_{z_{i}}B_{1}-1\leq d-2.

If
R_{2}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1})\neq 0
, we proceed as before Lemma
3.6
, and we obtain:

\mathrm{ord}_{\underline{x}}Q_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i})\leq\mathrm{ord}_{\underline{x}}R_{2}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1})\leq\omega\left(i-1,d_{x},\,d,\,d_{\underline{x}}{\mathbb{\delta}}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}((d-1)\mathbb{\delta}+(d-2)+1),\,d\mathbb{\delta}^{2}+\mathbb{\delta}((d-1)\mathbb{\delta}+(d-2)+1)\right).

Note that this new bound for
\mathrm{ord}_{\underline{x}}Q_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})
has increased with respect to the previous one, since
d\leq(d-1)(\mathbb{\delta}+1)=(d-1)\mathbb{\delta}+(d-2)+1
for any
d\geq 2
,
\mathbb{\delta}\geq 1
. At worst, one can have repeatedly the second case with successive Bézout identities:

A_{k}B_{k-1}+B_{k}Q_{i}=R_{k}

with
R_{k}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1})=0
where for
j=0,\ldots,i-1
,

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\deg_{\underline{x}}{R_{k}}&\leq&d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{k}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\left(\mathbb{\delta}^{k-1}(d-1)+{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-2}(d-2)+\cdots+{\mathbb{\delta}}(d-(k-1))+(d-k)+1\right)\\
\deg_{z_{j}}{R_{k}}&\leq&d{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k}+\mathbb{\delta}\left({\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-1}(d-1)+{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-2}(d-2)+\cdots+{\mathbb{\delta}}(d-(k-1))+(d-k)+1\right),\end{array}\right.

and with

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\deg_{\underline{x}}{B_{k}}&\leq&d_{\underline{x}}{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\left({\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-1}(d-1)+{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-2}(d-2)+\cdots+{\mathbb{\delta}}(d-k+1)+(d-k)\right)\\
\deg_{z_{j}}{B_{k}}&\leq&d{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k}+\mathbb{\delta}\left({\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-1}(d-1)+{\mathbb{\delta}}^{k-2}(d-2)+\cdots+{\mathbb{\delta}}(d-k+1)+(d-k)\right)\\
\deg_{z_{i}}B_{k}&\leq&d-k.\end{array}\right.

The greatest bound is obtained for
k=d-1
, for which
B_{d-1}
has
\deg_{z_{i}}B_{d-1}=1
. In this case,
B_{d-1}
has
c_{i}
as unique root and
Q_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})\neq 0
, so
R_{d}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1})\neq 0
. We set for
n,m\in\mathbb{N}^{*}
:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\phi(n,m)&:=&(n-1){m}^{n-1}+(n-2){m}^{n-2}+\cdots+{m}+1\\
&=&\left((n-1){m}^{n-2}+(n-2){m}^{n-3}+\cdots+2{m}+1\right)m+1\\
&=&\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)m^{n+1}-nm^{n}+m^{2}-m+1}{(m-1)^{2}}\textrm{ for }m\neq 1\end{array}

We have for
j=0,\ldots,i-1
:

\left\{\begin{array}[]{lcl}\deg_{\underline{x}}R_{d}&\leq&d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta})\\
\deg_{z_{j}}R_{d}&\leq&d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta}),\end{array}\right.

By the induction hypothesis,
\mathrm{ord}_{\underline{x}}R_{d}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1})
is bounded by

\omega\left(i-1,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta}),d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta})\right)
.
We get the corresponding expected bound:

\mathrm{ord}_{\underline{x}}Q_{i}(\underline{x},c_{0},\ldots,c_{i-1},c_{i})\leq\omega\left(i-1,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta}),d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta})\right),

which proves the existence of
\omega(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}},\mathbb{\delta})
with

(22)

\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}},\mathbb{\delta}\right)\leq\omega\left(i-1,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta}),d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta})\right).

To bound
\omega(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}},\mathbb{\delta})
, we need to find estimates for
\phi
.

First step: for
n,m\geq 2
,

\phi(n,m)\leq(n-1)m^{n}.

Indeed,
\phi(n,m)=\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)m^{n+1}-nm^{n}+m^{2}-m+1}{(m-1)^{2}}
. For
n\geq 2
,
-nm^{n}+m^{2}-m+1\leq 0
, so
\phi(n,m)\leq\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)m^{n+1}}{(m-1)^{2}}
and
\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)m^{n+1}}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq(n-1)m^{n}\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\frac{m}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq 1\Leftrightarrow m^{2}-3m+1\geq 0
with
\Delta=5
et
m=(3+\sqrt{5})/2<3
. This holds for
m\geq 3
. For
m=2
, we compute:

\phi(n,2)=(n-1)2^{n+1}-n2^{n}+3\leq(n-1)2^{n}\Leftrightarrow 3\leq 2^{n}

This holds for
n\geq 2
. On the other hand, this does not hold for
m=1
and
n\geq 3
.

Second step: for
n\geq 3
,
m\geq 2
,

(23)

\phi(n,m)\leq(2n-3)m^{n-1}

Indeed, from the first step:

\begin{array}[]{lcl}\phi(n,m):=(n-1){m}^{n-1}+(n-2){m}^{n-2}+\cdots+{m}+1&=&(n-1){m}^{n-1}+\phi(n-1,m)\\
&\leq&(n-1)m^{n-1}+(n-2)m^{n-1}\\
&\leq&(2n-3)m^{n-1}\end{array}

Let
\varepsilon>0
. For
n\geq 2
, since
-nm^{n}+m^{2}-m+1\leq 0
, the inequality

(24)

\phi(n,m)\leq(1+\varepsilon)(n-1)m^{n-1}

is implied by

\displaystyle\frac{(n-1)m^{n+1}}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq(1+\varepsilon)(n-1)m^{n-1}\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}\leq 1+\varepsilon.

This holds for
m
large enough, say for
m\geq m_{\varepsilon}
, since
\displaystyle\frac{m^{2}}{(m-1)^{2}}
decreases to 1.

Now, let us prove the estimates for
\omega(i,\ldots)
by induction on
i
. For
i=0
,
\omega(0,\ldots)\leq d\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}+\mathbb{\delta}d_{\underline{x}}
by Lemma
3.4
. Suppose that the estimates (
18
), (
19
), (
20
) and (
21
) hold until some
i\geq 0
. By (
22
):

\omega\left(i+1,d_{\underline{x}},d,\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}},\mathbb{\delta}\right)\leq\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta}),d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}\phi(d,\mathbb{\delta})\right)

\leq\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(2d-3)\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1},d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}(2d-3)\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1}\right)

\leq\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}2d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1},d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}2d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1}\right)

\leq\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}2d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1},3d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}\right)

\leq(2.3^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-2^{i}3^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}(3d\mathbb{\delta}^{d})^{d^{i}}+2^{i}.3^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)}d^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}(d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}2d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1})(3d\mathbb{\delta}^{d})^{d^{i}-1}

\leq(2.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-2^{i}3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+2^{i}.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)-1}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+2^{i+1}.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-2^{i}3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}2^{i}3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+2^{i+1}.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}2^{i}3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+2^{i+1}.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-2^{i+1}3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+2^{i+1}.3^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}.

This proves (
18
), and also (
19
) by letting
\mathbb{\delta}\leq d
and
\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\leq d_{\underline{x}}
.

Similarly, given
\varepsilon>0
, we use (
22
) and (
24
) with
\mathbb{\delta}\geq\mathbb{\delta}_{\varepsilon}
and, since
d-1<d
, we get:

\omega\left(i+1,d_{\underline{x}},d,\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}},\mathbb{\delta}\right)\leq\omega\left(i,d_{\underline{x}},d,d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(1+\varepsilon)d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1},(2+\varepsilon)d\mathbb{\delta}^{d}\right)

\leq(2.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}((2+\varepsilon)d\mathbb{\delta}^{d})^{d^{i}}+(1+\varepsilon)^{i}.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)}d^{d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}(d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d}+\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}(1+\varepsilon)d\mathbb{\delta}^{d-1})((2+\varepsilon)d\mathbb{\delta}^{d})^{d^{i}-1}

\leq(2.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+

(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)-1}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+

(1+\varepsilon)^{i+1}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2+\varepsilon)}(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+

(1+\varepsilon)^{i+1}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-\displaystyle\frac{(1+\varepsilon)}{(2+\varepsilon)}(1+\varepsilon)^{i}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-(i-1)})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+(1+\varepsilon)^{i+1}.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}

\leq(2.(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}-(1+\varepsilon)^{i+1}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i})d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+1}d_{x}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}}+

(1+\varepsilon)^{i+1}(2+\varepsilon)^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d-i}d^{d^{i}+d^{i-1}+\cdots+d^{2}+d+2}\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\mathbb{\delta}^{d^{i+1}-1}.

This proves (
20
), and also (
21
) by letting
\mathbb{\delta}\leq d
and
\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{x}}\leq d_{\underline{x}}
.

\Box

4.
Total reconstruction of vanishing polynomials for several algebraic series.

In the present section, we provide several improvements of
[
HM19
]
.

4.1.
Total reconstruction in the algebraic case.

Definition 4.1
.

•

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be two strictly increasing finite sequences of pairs
(\underline{k},j)\in\left(\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}\right)_{\textrm{alex}*}
ordered anti-lexicographically:

(\underline{k}_{1},j_{1})\leq_{\textrm{alex}*}(\underline{k}_{2},j_{2})\Leftrightarrow j_{1}<j_{2}\textrm{ or }(j_{1}=j_{2}\ \textrm{and}\ \underline{k}_{1}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\underline{k}_{2}).

We suppose additionally that
(\underline{k}_{1},j_{1})\geq_{\textrm{alex}*}\left(\underline{0},1\right)>_{\textrm{alex}*}(\underline{k}_{2},j_{2})
for any
(\underline{k}_{1},j_{1})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
(\underline{k}_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
(thus the elements of
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are ordered pairs of the form
(\underline{k}_{2},0)
, and those of
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
are of the form
(\underline{k}_{1},j_{1}),\ j_{1}\geq 1
).

We denote
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}:=\max\{j,\ (\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\}
and
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}:=\max\{|\underline{k}|,\ (\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\}
.

•

We say that a series
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[[\underline{s}]]
is
algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
if there exists a polynomial
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=0
.

•

Let
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime},d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}\geq 1
. We say that a series
y_{0}^{\prime}\in K[[\underline{s}]]
is
algebraic of degrees bounded by
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}
and
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}
if it is algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
where
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are the complete sequences of indices
(\underline{k},j)\in\left(\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}\right)_{\textrm{alex}*}
with
j\leq d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}
and
|\underline{k}|\leq d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}
.

Let us consider a series
Y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}C_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[(C_{\underline{m}})_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}][[\underline{s}]]
where
\underline{s}
and the
C_{\underline{m}}
’s are variables. We denote the multinomial expansion of the
j
th power
{Y_{0}^{\prime}}^{j}
of
Y_{0}^{\prime}
by:

{Y_{0}^{\prime}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}C_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}.

where
C_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}\in K[(C_{\underline{m}})_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}]
.
For instance, one has that
C_{\underline{0}}^{(j)}={C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}
. For
j=0
, we set
{Y_{0}^{\prime}}^{0}:=1
. More generally, for any
\underline{m}
and any
j\leq|\underline{m}|
,
C_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}
is a homogeneous polynomial of degree
j
in the
C_{\underline{k}}
’s for
\underline{k}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}
,
\underline{k}\leq\underline{m}
, with coefficients in
\mathbb{N}^{*}
.

Now suppose we are given a series
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[[\underline{s}]]\setminus\{0\}
. For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote the multinomial expansion of
{y_{0}^{\prime}}^{j}
by:

{y_{0}^{\prime}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}.

So,
c_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}=C_{\underline{m}}^{(j)}(c_{\underline{0}},\ldots,c_{\underline{m}})
.

Definition 4.2
.

Let
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[[\underline{s}]]\setminus\{0\}
.

(1)

Given a pair
(\underline{k},j)\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}
, we call
Wilczynski vector

V_{\underline{k},j}
(associated to
y_{0}^{\prime}
) the infinite vector with components
\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},j}
with
\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}
ordered with
\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}
:

- if
j\geq 1
:

V_{\underline{k},j}:=\left(\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},j}\right)_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}\textrm{ with }\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},j}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}=c_{\underline{m}-\underline{k}}^{(j)}&\textrm{ if }\underline{m}\geq\underline{k}\\
=0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.

- otherwise: 1 in the
\underline{k}
th position and 0 for the other coefficients,

V_{\underline{k},0}:=(0,\ldots,1,0,0,\ldots,0,\ldots).

So
\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},j}
is the coefficient of
s^{\underline{m}}
in the expansion of
s^{\underline{k}}{y_{0}^{\prime}}^{j}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be two sequences as in Definition
4.1
. We associate to
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
y_{0}^{\prime}
the
(infinite) Wilczynski matrix
whose columns are the corresponding vectors
V_{\underline{k},j}
:

M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}:=(V_{\underline{k},j})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\,,

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
being ordered by
\leq_{\textrm{alex}*}
as in Definition
4.1
.

We also define the
reduced Wilczynski matrix
,
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
: it is the matrix obtained from
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
by removing the columns indexed in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, and also removing the corresponding rows (suppress the
\underline{k}
th row for any
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
). This amounts exactly to remove the rows containing the coefficient 1 for some Wilczynski vector indexed in
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. For
(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, we also denote by
V_{\underline{i},j}^{red}
the corresponding vectors obtained from
V_{\underline{i},j}
by suppressing the
\underline{k}
th row for any
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and we call them
reduced Wilczynski vectors
.

The following result is
[
HM19
, Lemma 3.2]
:

Lemma 4.3

(generalized Wilczynski)
.

The series
y_{0}^{\prime}
is algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
if and only if all the minors of order
|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}|
of the Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
vanish, or also if and only if all the minors of order
|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|
of the reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
vanish.

Let us give an outline of the reconstruction process of
[
HM19
]
. Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
be two sequences as in Definition
4.1
and
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[[\underline{s}]]\setminus\{0\}
be algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Our purpose is to describe the
K
-vector space whose non-zero elements are the polynomials
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=0
. The components of the infinite vector computed as
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},j})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
are exactly the coefficients of the expansion of
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})
in
K[[\underline{s}]]
.
Let us now remark that, in the infinite vector
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},j})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}
, if we remove the components indexed by
\underline{k}
for
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, then we get exactly the infinite vector
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},j})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
. The vanishing of the latter means precisely that the rank of
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
is less than
|\mathcal{F}|
.
Conversely, if the columns of
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
are dependent for certain
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
, we denote by
(a_{\underline{k},j})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
a corresponding sequence of coefficients of a nontrivial vanishing linear combination of the column vectors. Then it suffices to note that the remaining coefficients
a_{\underline{k},0}
for
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are uniquely determined as follows:

(25)

a_{\underline{k},0}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},j}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}\,.

We consider a maximal family
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
such that the corresponding reduced Wilczynski vectors are
K
-linearly independent. Proceeding as in Lemma 3.7 in
[
HM19
]
,
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
is such a family if and only if, in the reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
, there is a nonzero minor
\det(A)
where
A
has columns indexed in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
and lowest row with index
\underline{m}
such that
|\underline{m}|\leq 2d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
is maximal with this property. Moreover, among such
A
’s, we take one that has its lowest row having an index minimal for
\leq_{\mathrm{grlex}}
, and we denote the latter index by
\hat{\underline{p}}
.

For any
(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
, the family of reduced Wilczynski vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})
with
(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\}
is
K
-linearly dependent. There is a unique relation:

(26)

V_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}^{red}=\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}\ \ \textrm{ with }\ \ \lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\in K.

We consider the restriction of
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
to the rows of
A
. For these rows, by Cramer’s rule, we reconstruct the linear combination (
26
).
The coefficients
\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}
of such a linear combination are quotients of homogeneous polynomials with integer coefficients in terms of the entries of these restricted matrix, hence quotients of polynomials in the corresponding
c_{\underline{m}}
’s,
|\underline{m}|\leq 2d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}
.

Let
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
. One has
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=0
if and only if (
25
) holds as well as:

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}V_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}^{red}=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\left(\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}\right)=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\left(a_{\underline{k},j}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\right)V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\forall(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},j}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}},

Lemma 4.4
.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime},d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime},d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime},y_{0}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
be as above. Then, the
K
-vector space of polynomials
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=0
is the set of polynomials such that

(27)

\forall(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},j}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}},

and

(28)

\forall(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},0}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},j}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}\,,

where the
\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}
’s are computed as in (
26
) as quotients of polynomials with integer coefficients in the
c_{\underline{m}}
’s for
|\underline{m}|\leq 2d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}
.

Remark 4.5
.

Note that the set of polynomials
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]
with support in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=0
is a
K
-vector space of dimension
|\mathcal{F}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}|\geq 1
.

4.2.
Total algebraic reconstruction in the non-homogeneous case.

Let
\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime},d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime},d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}
be as in Definition
4.1
.

4.2.1.
First case.

Let
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K[[\underline{s}]]
be
algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
. Let
i,\,d_{\underline{s}},\,d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
d^{\prime}\geq 3
,
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\leq d_{\underline{s}}
and
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}
. For any
j=0,\ldots,i
, we consider power series
y_{j}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{j,\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K\left[\left[\underline{s}\right]\right]

which satisfy some equations
{P}_{j}(\underline{s},y^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{j})=0
where
P_{j}\in K\left[\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}\right]\setminus\{0\}
,
P_{j}(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{j-1}^{\prime},z_{j})\not\equiv 0
,
\deg_{\underline{s}}P_{j}\leq d_{\underline{s}},

\deg_{z_{k}}{P}_{j}\leq d^{\prime}
for
k=0,\ldots,j
. In particular,
c_{\underline{m}}=c_{0,\underline{m}}
for any
\underline{m}
.
Let
z^{\prime}=R(\underline{s},y^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i})\in K[[\underline{s}]]\setminus\{0\}
, where
R\in K\left[\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{i}\right]\setminus\{0\}
with
\deg_{\underline{s}}R\leq d_{\underline{s}},

\deg_{z_{k}}R\leq d^{\prime}
for
k=0,\ldots,i
.

We want to determine when there is a polynomial
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=z^{\prime}
and, subsequently, to reconstruct all such possible
P
’s.

Let
V
be the infinite vector with components the coefficients of
z^{\prime}
, and
V^{red}
the corresponding reduced vector as in Definition
4.2
. For
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
as in the previous section, we have
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=z^{\prime}
if and only if:

(29)

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\left(a_{\underline{k},j}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\right)V_{\underline{k},j}^{red}=V^{red}.

We want to examine when the vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent. Let
N^{red}
be the infinite matrix with columns
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
.

Lemma 4.6
.

The vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent if and only if all the minors of maximal order of
N^{red}
up to the row
\underline{p}
with:

|\underline{p}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}

vanish.

Proof.

The vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent if and only if all the minors of
N^{red}
of maximal order vanish: see
[
HM17
, Lemma 1]
.

Conversely, we suppose that the vectors are linearly independent. So, there is a minor of
N
of maximal order which is nonzero. Let
\underline{p}
be the smallest multi-index for
\leq_{\mathrm{grlex}}
such that there is such a nonzero minor of
N^{red}
of maximal order with lowest row of index
\underline{p}
. Hence, there is a subminor of it based on the columns indexed in
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
which is nonzero, say
\det(B)
. The lowest row of
B
is at most
\underline{p}
. So, by minimality of
\hat{\underline{p}}
(see before (
26
) in the previous section),
\underline{p}\geq_{\mathrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{p}}
. If
\underline{p}=\hat{\underline{p}}
, then
|\underline{p}|\leq 2d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}d^{\prime}
and we are done. If
\underline{p}>_{\mathrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{p}}
, let us denote by
\underline{\tilde{p}}
the predecessor of
\underline{p}
for
\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}
. Then
\tilde{\underline{p}}\geq_{\mathrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{p}}
.
For any multi-index
\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{r}
, denote by
N_{\underline{m}}^{red},V_{\underline{k},j,\underline{m}}^{red},V_{\underline{m}}^{red}
the truncations up to the row
\underline{m}
of
N^{red},V_{\underline{k},j}^{red},V^{red}
respectively. By definition of
\underline{p}
, the rank of the matrix
N^{red}_{\underline{p}}
is
|\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}|+1
, whereas the rank of
N^{red}_{\underline{\tilde{p}}}
is
|\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}|
. There exists a nonzero vector
((a_{\underline{i},j})_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}},-a)
of elements of
K
such that

(30)

N_{\underline{\tilde{p}}}^{red}\,\cdot\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(a_{\underline{i},j})_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\\
-a\end{array}\right)=0,

where
a
can be chosen to be 1 since the vectors
\left(V_{\underline{k},j,\underline{\tilde{p}}}^{red}\right)_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
are independent. The components of the resulting vector
N_{\underline{\tilde{p}}}^{red}\,\cdot\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(a_{\underline{i},j})_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\\
-1\end{array}\right)
are exactly the coefficients
e_{\underline{k}}
,
(\underline{k},0)\notin\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
and
\underline{k}\leq_{\mathrm{grlex}}\underline{\tilde{p}}
, of the expansion of
\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{i},j}\,\underline{s}^{\underline{i}}\,(y_{0}^{\prime})^{j}-z^{\prime}
.
By computing the coefficients
a_{\underline{k},0}
for
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
as:

(31)

a_{\underline{k},0}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\underline{k}>\underline{i}}a_{\underline{i},j}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}+f_{\underline{k}},

where
f_{\underline{k}}
denotes the coefficient of
{\underline{s}}^{\underline{k}}
in
z^{\prime}
,
we obtain the vanishing of the first terms of
Q(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i}):=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{i},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{i}}(y_{0}^{\prime})^{j}-z^{\prime}
up to
\tilde{\underline{p}}
. So,
w_{\underline{s}}(Q(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i}))\geq_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{p}}
and, therefore,
\ord(Q(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i}))\geq|{\underline{p}}|
.

On the contrary,
we have:

(32)

N_{\underline{p}}^{red}\,\cdot\,\left(\begin{array}[]{c}(a_{\underline{i},j})_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}\\
-1\end{array}\right)\neq 0.

From (
30
) and (
32
), we deduce that the coefficient
e_{\underline{p}}
of
\underline{s}^{\underline{p}}
in the expansion of

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{i},j}\,\underline{x}^{\underline{i}}\,(y_{0}^{\prime})^{j}-z^{\prime}
is nonzero.
Observe that this term of the latter series does not overlap with the terms of
\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{i},0}\,\underline{s}^{\underline{i}}
since
(\underline{p},0)\notin\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. Therefore,
w_{\underline{s}}(Q(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i}))={\underline{p}}
. In particular,
Q(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i})\neq 0
, so the bound (
19
) in Theorem
3.5
applies:

|\underline{p}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}.

∎

Let us return to (
29
). Let
A
be the square matrix defined after (
26
). For any
(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
, we denote by
A_{\underline{k},j}
the matrix deduced from
A
by substituting the corresponding part of
V^{red}
instead of the column indexed by
(\underline{k},j)
. Equality (
29
) holds if and only if the vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent, and by Cramer’s rule, one has:

(33)

\forall(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},j}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}=\frac{\det(A_{\underline{k},j})}{\det(A)}.

Recall that one determines that
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent by examining the dependence of the finite truncation of these vectors according to Lemma
4.6
. Finally, the remaining coefficients
a_{\underline{k},0}
for
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are each uniquely determined as follows:

(34)

a_{\underline{k},0}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},j}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}+f_{\underline{k}}\,,

where
f_{\underline{k}}
denotes the coefficient of
{\underline{s}}^{\underline{k}}
in
z^{\prime}
.

As a conclusion, we obtain the affine space of
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=z^{\prime}
as a parametric family of its coefficients with free parameters the
a_{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}
’s for
(\underline{k}_{0},j_{0})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
.

4.2.2.
Second case.

Let
\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and
y_{0}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K\left[\left[\underline{s}\right]\right]
be algebraic of degrees
d^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
and
\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}
,
but not algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.
Let
i,\,d_{\underline{s}},\,d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
d^{\prime}\geq 3
,
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\leq d_{\underline{s}}
and
d_{y^{\prime}}^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}
. For any
j=0,\ldots,i
, we consider power series
y_{j}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{j,\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K\left[\left[\underline{s}\right]\right]

which satisfy some equations
{P}_{j}(\underline{s},y^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{j})=0
where
P_{j}\in K\left[\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}\right]\setminus\{0\}
,
P_{j}(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{j-1}^{\prime},z_{j})\not\equiv 0
,
\deg_{\underline{s}}P_{j}\leq d_{\underline{s}},

\deg_{z_{k}}{P}_{j}\leq d^{\prime}
for
k=0,\ldots,j
. In particular,
c_{\underline{m}}=c_{0,\underline{m}}
for any
\underline{m}
.
Let
z^{\prime}=R(\underline{s},y^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{i})\in K[[\underline{s}]]\setminus\{0\}
, where
R\in K\left[\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}\right]\setminus\{0\}
with
\deg_{\underline{s}}R\leq d_{\underline{s}},

\deg_{z_{k}}R\leq d^{\prime}
for
k=0,\ldots,j
.

As in the previous section, our purpose is to determine when there is a polynomial
P(\underline{s},y^{\prime})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{y^{\prime}}^{j}\in K[\underline{s},y^{\prime}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=z^{\prime}
. Note that such a polynomial is necessarily unique, since
y_{0}^{\prime}
is not algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime})
.

We consider the corresponding reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}}^{red}
. Proceeding as in Lemma 3.7 in
[
HM19
]
and using Lemma
3.4
, there is a nonzero minor
\det(B)
of maximal order where the lowest row of
B
is indexed by
\underline{m}
such that
|\underline{m}|\leq\left(\mathbb{\delta}_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}+d^{\prime}_{\underline{s}}\right)d^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
.

We resume the notations of the previous section. There is a polynomial
P
such that
P(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime})=z^{\prime}
if and only if the vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are
K
-linearly dependent, since the vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
are independent. One determines that
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent by examining the dependence of the finite truncation of these vectors according to the following lemma.

Lemma 4.7
.

The vectors
(V_{\underline{k},j}^{red})_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}}
and
V^{red}
are linearly dependent if and only if, in the corresponding matrix denoted by
N^{red}
, all the minors of maximal order up to the row
\underline{p}
with
|\underline{p}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}
vanish.

Proof.

The proof is analogous to that of Lemma
4.6
, also using Theorem
3.5
.
∎

We proceed as in the previous section. For any
(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}
, we denote by
B_{\underline{k},j}
the matrix deduced from
B
by substituting the corresponding part of
V^{red}
instead of the column indexed by
(\underline{k},j)
. If the condition of the previous lemma holds, by Cramer’s rule, one has:

(35)

\forall(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},j}=\frac{\det(B_{\underline{k},j})}{\det(B)}.

Then it suffices to note that the remaining coefficients
a_{\underline{k},0}
for
(\underline{k},0)\in\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
are each uniquely determined as follows:

(36)

a_{\underline{k},0}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},j}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}+f_{\underline{k}}\,,

where
f_{\underline{k}}
denotes the coefficient of
{\underline{s}}^{\underline{k}}
in
z^{\prime}
.

4.3.
Total algebraic reconstruction with several algebraic series.

Let
i,\,d_{\underline{s}},\,d^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
,
d^{\prime}\geq 3
. For any
j=0,\ldots,i
, we consider power series
y_{j}^{\prime}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{j,\underline{m}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\in K\left[\left[\underline{s}\right]\right]

which satisfy some equations
{P}_{j}(\underline{s},y^{\prime}_{0},\ldots,y^{\prime}_{j})=0
where
P_{j}\in K\left[\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1},\ldots,z_{j}\right]\setminus\{0\}
,
P_{j}(\underline{s},y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{j-1}^{\prime},z_{j})\not\equiv 0
,
\deg_{\underline{s}}P_{j}\leq d_{\underline{s}},

\deg_{z_{k}}{P}_{j}\leq d^{\prime}
for
k=0,\ldots,j
.

Let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
,
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
, be two strictly increasing finite sequences of pairs
(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\left(\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}^{i+1}\right)
ordered anti-lexicographically:

(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1})\leq_{\textrm{alex}*}(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2})\Leftrightarrow\underline{l}_{1}<_{\textrm{grlex}}\underline{l}_{2}\textrm{ or }(\underline{l}_{1}=\underline{l}_{2}\ \textrm{and}\ \underline{k}_{1}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\underline{k}_{2}).

We suppose additionally that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\geq_{\textrm{alex}*}\left(\underline{0},(0,\ldots,0,1)\right)>_{\textrm{alex}*}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
(thus the elements of
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are ordered tuples of the form
(\underline{k},\underline{0})
, and those of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
are of the form
(\underline{k},\underline{l}),\ |\underline{l}|\geq 1
).

We set
d_{y^{\prime}_{j}}^{\prime}:=\max\{l_{j},\ (\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\}
for
j=0,\ldots,i
, and
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}:=\max\{|\underline{k}|,\ (\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}\}
. We assume that
d_{y^{\prime}_{j}}^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}
for
j=0,\ldots,i
, and
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\leq d_{\underline{s}}
.

Let us set
\underline{z}=(z_{0},\ldots,z_{i})
and
\underline{y}^{\prime}=(y_{0}^{\prime},\ldots,y_{i}^{\prime})
. We assume that
\underline{y}^{\prime}\neq\underline{0}
. We want to determine when there is a polynomial
P(\underline{s},\underline{z})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{\underline{z}}^{\underline{l}}\in K[\underline{s},\underline{z}]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})=0
and, subsequently, to reconstruct all such possible
P
’s. It is a generalization of Section
4.1
.

For any
j=0,\ldots,i
, for any
l_{j}\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote the multinomial expansion of
{y_{j}^{\prime}}^{l_{j}}
by:

{y_{j}^{\prime}}^{l_{j}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}_{j}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}c_{j,\underline{n}_{j}}^{(l_{j})}\underline{s}^{\underline{n}_{j}}.

So the coefficient of
\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}
in
\underline{y}^{\prime\underline{l}}={y_{0}^{\prime}}^{l_{0}}\cdots{y_{i}^{\prime}}^{l_{i}}
is equal to:

c_{\underline{m}}^{(\underline{l})}:=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\ldots,\underline{n}_{i}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\ \underline{n}_{0}+\cdots+\underline{n}_{i}=\underline{m}}c_{0,\underline{n}_{0}}^{(l_{0})}\cdots c_{i,\underline{n}_{i}}^{(l_{i})}.

Definition 4.8
.

(1)

Given an ordered pair
(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}^{i+1}
, we call
Wilczynski vector

V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}
the infinite vector with components
\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},\underline{l}}
with
\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}
ordered with
\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}
:

- if
\underline{l}\geq_{\mathrm{grlex}}(0,\ldots,0,1)
:

V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}:=\left(\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},\underline{l}}\right)_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}\textrm{ with }\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},\underline{l}}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}=c_{\underline{m}-\underline{k}}^{(\underline{l})}&\textrm{ if }\underline{m}\geq\underline{k}\\
=0&\textrm{ otherwise }\end{array}\right.

- otherwise: 1 in the
\underline{k}
th position and 0 for the other coefficients,

V_{\underline{k},\underline{0}}:=(0,\ldots,1,0,0,\ldots,0,\ldots).

So
\gamma_{\underline{m}}^{\underline{k},\underline{l}}
is the coefficient of
s^{\underline{m}}
in the expansion of
s^{\underline{k}}{\underline{y}^{\prime}}^{\underline{l}}
.

(2)

Let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
be two sequences as above. We associate to
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
the
(infinite) Wilczynski matrix
whose columns are the corresponding vectors
V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}
:

M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}:=(V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\,,

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
being ordered by
\leq_{\textrm{alex}*}
as above.

We also define the
reduced Wilczynski matrix
,
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}
: it is the matrix obtained from
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
by removing the columns indexed in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, and also removing the corresponding rows (suppress the
\underline{k}
th row for any
(\underline{k},\underline{0})\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
). This amounts exactly to remove the rows containing the coefficient 1 for some Wilczynski vector indexed in
\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. For
(\underline{i},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
, we also denote by
V_{\underline{i},\underline{l}}^{red}
the corresponding vectors obtained from
V_{\underline{i},\underline{l}}
by suppressing the
\underline{k}
th row for any
(\underline{k},\underline{0})\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
and we call them
reduced Wilczynski vectors
.

Lemma 4.9

(generalized Wilczynski)
.

There exists a nonzero polynomial with support included in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
which vanishes at
\underline{y}^{\prime}
if and only if all the minors of order
|\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}|
of the Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
vanish, or also if and only if all the minors of order
|\mathcal{K}^{\prime}|
of the reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}
vanish.

Proof
.

By construction of the Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
, the existence of such a polynomial is equivalent to the fact that the corresponding Wilczynski vectors are
K
-linearly dependent. This is in turn equivalent to the vanishing of all the minors of maximal order of
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
.

Suppose that we are given a nonzero vector
(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
such that

M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}=0.

Observe that, necessarily, the vector
(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
is also nonzero (since the vectors
V_{\underline{k},\underline{0}}
for
(\underline{k},\underline{0})\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
are independent). Let us remark that:

M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}=0

since the latter vector is deduced from the former one by deleting the rows corresponding to
(\underline{k},\underline{0})\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
. So, the columns of
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}
are linked, which is equivalent to the vanishing of its minors of maximal order. Conversely, suppose that there exists a nonzero
(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}
such that

M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}\cdot(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}}=0.

Then, we can complete the list of coefficients
(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}
by setting:

(37)

a_{\underline{k},\underline{0}}=-\sum_{(\underline{i},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},\underline{l}}\,c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(\underline{l})}.

\Box

Lemma 4.10
.

There exists a nonzero polynomial with support included in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
which vanishes at
\underline{y}^{\prime}
if and only if all the minors of the reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}
of order
|\mathcal{K}^{\prime}|
and with lowest row indexed by
\underline{m}
with:

|\underline{m}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1},

vanish.

Proof
.

The direct part follows from the previous lemma. Suppose that there is no nonzero polynomial with support included in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
which vanishes at
\underline{y}^{\prime}
. So there is a nonzero minor of the reduced Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}^{red}
of order
|\mathcal{K}^{\prime}|
and with lowest row indexed by
\underline{m}
that we assume to be minimal for
\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}
. Reasoning as in the proof of Lemma
4.6
, we obtain a nonzero polynomial
Q(\underline{s},z_{0},\ldots,z_{i})
with
\textrm{Supp}(Q)\subseteq\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
, such that
Q(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})\neq 0
, and with
\ord_{\underline{s}}\left(Q(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})\right)\geq|\underline{m}|
. Since
d_{y^{\prime}_{j}}^{\prime}\leq d^{\prime}
for
j=0,\ldots,i
, and
d_{\underline{s}}^{\prime}\leq d_{\underline{s}}
, by Theorem
3.5
, we obtain that:

\ord_{\underline{s}}\left(Q(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})\right)\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1},

which gives the expected result.

\Box

Let us suppose that there is a nonzero polynomial
P
with support included in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
which vanishes at
\underline{y}^{\prime}
. Our purpose is to determine the space of all such polynomials.
For this, we consider a maximal family
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}\subsetneq\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
such that the corresponding reduced Wilczynski vectors are
K
-linearly independent. This is equivalent to the fact that, for the matrix consisting of the
(V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red})
with
(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
, there is a nonzero minor
\det(A)
of maximal order and with lowest row indexed by
\underline{m}
with

|\underline{m}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}.

For any
(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
, the corresponding family of reduced Wilczynski vectors
(V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red})
with
(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\cup\{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\}
is
K
-linearly dependent. There is a unique relation:

(38)

V_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}^{red}=\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red}\ \ \textrm{ with }\ \ \lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\in K.

which can be computed by Cramer’s rule based on
\det(A)
.
The coefficients
\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}
of such a linear combination are quotients of homogeneous polynomials with integer coefficients in terms of the entries of these restricted matrices, hence quotients of polynomials in the corresponding
c_{\underline{m}}
’s,
|\underline{m}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}
.

Let
\underline{z}=(z_{0},\ldots,z_{i})
, and
P(\underline{s},\underline{z})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{\underline{z}}^{\underline{l}}\in K[\underline{s},\underline{z}]\setminus\{0\}
. One has
P(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})=0
if and only if (
37
) holds as well as:

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}V_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}^{red}=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\left(\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red}\right)=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}\left(a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}+\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\right)V_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{red}=\underline{0}

\Leftrightarrow\forall(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\mathcal{L}^{\prime},d_{\underline{s}},d^{\prime},\underline{y}^{\prime},\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
be as above. Then, the set of polynomials
P(\underline{s},\underline{z})=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{\underline{z}}^{\underline{l}}\in K[\underline{s},\underline{z}]
such that
P(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})=0
is the set of polynomials such that

(39)

\forall(\underline{k},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\setminus\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}}a_{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}},

and

(40)

\forall(\underline{k},\underline{0})\in\mathcal{L}^{\prime},\ \ a_{\underline{k},\underline{0}}=-\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{i},\underline{l})\in\mathcal{K}^{\prime},\,\underline{i}\leq\underline{k}}a_{\underline{i},\underline{l}}c_{\underline{k}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}\,,

where the
\lambda_{\underline{k},\underline{l}}^{\underline{k}_{0},\underline{l}_{0}}
’s are computed as in (
38
) as quotients of polynomials with integer coefficients in the
c_{\underline{m}}
’s for
|\underline{m}|\leq 2.3^{(d^{\prime})^{i-1}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}d_{\underline{s}}(d^{\prime})^{(d^{\prime})^{i}+\cdots+(d^{\prime})^{2}+d^{\prime}+1}
.

Remark 4.12
.

Note that the set of polynomials
P(\underline{s},\underline{z})\in K[\underline{s},\underline{z}]
with support in
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\cup\mathcal{L}^{\prime}
such that
P(\underline{s},\underline{y}^{\prime})=0
is a
K
-vector space of dimension
|\mathcal{K}^{\prime}|-|\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}|\geq 1
.

5.
Reconstruction of an equation for an algebroid series.

5.1.
The reconstruction algorithm

We resume the notations of Section
2
, in particular Lemma
2.5
and after. In particular, recall that
\tau
is the number of variables in
\underline{s}
, and so
r-\tau
is the number of variables in
\underline{t}
.

Definition 5.1
.

Let
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{G}
be two strictly increasing sequences of triples
(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}\times\mathbb{N}
ordered as follows:

(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1},j_{1})\leq_{*\textrm{alex}*}(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2},j_{2}):\Leftrightarrow j_{1}<j_{2}\textrm{ or }(j_{1}=j_{2}\ \textrm{and}\ (\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1})\leq_{\textrm{alex}{*}}(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2}))

with

(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1})\leq_{\textrm{alex}{*}}(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2}):\Leftrightarrow\underline{l}_{1}<_{\mathrm{grlex}}\underline{l}_{2}\textrm{ or }(\underline{l}_{1}=\underline{l}_{2}\ \textrm{and}\ \underline{k}_{1}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\underline{k}_{2}).

We suppose additionally that
(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1},j_{1})\geq_{*\textrm{alex}*}\left(\underline{0},\underline{0},1\right)>_{*\textrm{alex}*}(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2},j_{2})
for any
(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1},j_{1})\in\mathcal{F}
and
(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2},j_{2})\in\mathcal{G}
(thus the elements of
\mathcal{G}
are ordered triples of the form
(\underline{k}_{2},\underline{l}_{2},0)
, and those of
\mathcal{F}
are of the form
(\underline{k}_{1},\underline{l}_{1},j_{1}),\ j_{1}\geq 1
). Moreover, we assume that there is
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
d\geq 1
, such that
j\leq d
for any
(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}
, and we set
d:=\max\{j\ |\,\exists(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}\}
. We say that a series
y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{m},\underline{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}\in K[[\underline{s},{\underline{t}}]]
,
c_{\underline{0},\underline{0}}\neq 0
, is
algebroid relatively to
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
if there exists a polynomial
P(\underline{s},\underline{t},y)=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}\underline{t}^{\underline{l}}y^{j}\in K[[\underline{s},\,\underline{t}]][y]\setminus\{0\}
such that
P(\underline{s},\underline{t},y_{0})=0
.

For any
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
satisfying Conditions (i), (ii), (iii) of Lemma
2.5
, let us denote by

\left(K[\underline{s}][[\underline{t}]][y]\right)_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
the subset of polynomials in
K[\underline{s}][[\underline{t}]][y]\setminus\{0\}
with support in
\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}
.

The purpose of the following discussion is to make more explicit the conditions in Lemma
2.8
for the vanishing of a polynomial
P\in\left(K[\underline{s}][[\underline{t}]][y]\right)_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}
for some
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
corresponding to (i), (ii), (iii) in Lemma
2.5
, at a formal power series
y_{0}\in K[[\underline{s}]][[\underline{t}]]
. As we have seen in Section
2
, one can always assume that
y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\,\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}
is such that
c_{\underline{0},\underline{0}}\neq 0
.

Let us consider a series

Y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}\,\left(\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\right)\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}C_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}\in K[(C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}})_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\,\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}][[\underline{s}]][[\underline{t}]]

where
\underline{s}
,
\underline{t}
and the
C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}
’s are variables. We denote the multinomial expansion of the
j
th power
{Y_{0}}^{j}
of
Y_{0}
by:

{Y_{0}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}\,\left(\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}}C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}^{(j)}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\right)\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}C_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}

where
C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}^{(j)}\in K\left[(C_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{\underline{k}\leq\underline{m},\,\underline{l}\leq\underline{n}}\right]
and

C_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\in K\left[\left(C_{\underline{l}}(\underline{s})\right)_{\underline{l}\leq\underline{n}}\right]\subseteq K\left[(C_{\underline{k},\underline{l}})_{\underline{k}\leq\underline{m},\,\underline{l}\leq_{\mathrm{grlex}}\underline{n}}\right][[\underline{s}]].

We also set
{Y_{0}}^{0}:=1
.

Now, suppose we are given a series
y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\,\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}\in K[[\underline{s},\underline{t}]]
with
c_{\underline{0},\underline{0}}\neq 0
. For any
j\in\mathbb{N}
, we denote the multinomial expansion of
{y_{0}}^{j}
by:

(41)

{y_{0}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\,\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}^{(j)}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}.

So,
c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}^{(j)}=C_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}^{(j)}\left(c_{\underline{0},\underline{0}},\ldots,c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\right)
and
c_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})=C_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}\left(c_{\underline{0}}(\underline{s}),\ldots,c_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})\right)
. We also set
{y_{0}}^{0}:=1
.

Lemma 5.2
.

For a polynomial
P\in\left(K[\underline{s}][[\underline{t}]][y]\right)_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}\setminus\{0\}
, we denote

P(\underline{s},\underline{t},y)=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}\underline{t}^{\underline{l}}y^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau},\,j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{l},j}(\underline{s})\underline{t}^{\underline{l}}y^{j}.

A series
y_{0}\in K[[\underline{s}]][[\underline{t}]]
,
y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau},\,\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}
, is a root of
P
if and only if the following polynomial relations hold when evaluated at the series
c_{\underline{0}}(\underline{s}),\ldots,c_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})
:

(42)

\forall\underline{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau},\ \displaystyle\sum_{j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{l},j}(\underline{s}){C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}(\underline{s})=-\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{i}<\underline{l},\,j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{i},j}(\underline{s})C_{\underline{l}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\,.

Proof
.

Let us compute:

P(\underline{s},\underline{t},y_{0})=\displaystyle\sum_{{\underline{i}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau},\,j=0,..,d}a_{{\underline{i}},j}(\underline{s})\underline{t}^{{\underline{i}}}{y_{0}}^{j}

=\displaystyle\sum_{{\underline{i}}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau},\,j=0,..,d}a_{{\underline{i}},j}(\underline{s})\underline{t}^{{\underline{i}}}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{n}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}\right)

=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}\left(\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{i}\leq\underline{l},\,j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{i},j}(\underline{s})c_{\underline{l}-\underline{i}}^{(j)}(\underline{s})\right)\underline{t}^{\underline{l}}.

So,
y_{0}
is a root of
P
if and only if, in the latter formula, the coefficient of
\underline{t}^{\underline{l}}
for each
\underline{l}
vanishes, which is equivalent to the vanishing of (
42
) (noticing that
C_{\underline{0}}^{(j)}={C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}
for all
j
).

\Box

Let
\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}
be as in Definition
5.1
and satisfying Conditions (i), (ii), (iii) of Lemma
2.5
. Let
y_{0}=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{m},\underline{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{m},\underline{n}}\underline{s}^{\underline{m}}\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}=\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{n}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}}c_{\underline{n}}(\underline{s})\,\underline{t}^{\underline{n}}\in K[[\underline{s},{\underline{t}}]]
,
c_{\underline{0},\underline{0}}\neq 0
, be a series algebroid relatively to
(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G})
. Let
P\in\left(K[\underline{s}][[\underline{t}]][y]\right)_{\mathcal{F},\mathcal{G}}\setminus\{0\}
be a polynomial such that
P(\underline{s},\underline{t},y_{0})=0
.
We notice that
w_{\underline{t}}(P)
is the index of the first non-trivial relation (
42
), for
\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}
ordered with
\leq_{\mathrm{grlex}}
. Let
\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}
be such that
w_{\underline{t}}(P)\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
. If
w_{\underline{t}}(P)
is known, then one can take
\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}=w_{\underline{t}}(P)
.

5.1.1.
First step

For any
\underline{l}\in\mathbb{N}^{r-\tau}
, we denote by
{\mathcal{F}}_{\underline{l}}^{\prime}
and
{\mathcal{G}}_{\underline{l}}^{\prime}
the corresponding sets of tuples
(\underline{k},j)\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}\times\mathbb{N}
where
(\underline{k},\underline{l},j)\in\mathcal{F}
and
(\underline{k},\underline{l},0)\in\mathcal{G}
respectively. We denote
d^{\prime}_{\underline{s},\underline{l}}:=\max\{|\underline{k}|\ |\,(\underline{k},j)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\underline{l}}^{\prime}\cup{\mathcal{G}}_{\underline{l}}^{\prime}\}
(which is well-defined thanks to Condition (iii) of Lemma
2.5
). By (
17
) in Remark
2.10
, we have that:

d^{\prime}_{\underline{s},\underline{l}}\leq a|\underline{l}|+b,

where
a
and
b
are as in Lemma
2.9
.

Let
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
(or directly
\underline{l}=w_{\underline{t}}(P)
if known). As we are interested in the first non trivial relation in (
42
), we consider its following instance:

(43)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{l},j}(\underline{s}){C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{C_{0}}^{j}=0\,.

By Lemma
5.2
, there is
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
c_{\underline{0}}
satisfies the latter relation, i.e.
c_{\underline{0}}
is algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}})
. In particular,
c_{\underline{0}}
is algebraic relatively to
\left(\displaystyle\bigcup_{\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}}\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\displaystyle\bigcup_{\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}}\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\right)
. We denote
d^{\prime}_{\underline{s}}:=\displaystyle\max_{\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}}\left(d^{\prime}_{\underline{s},\underline{l}}\right)
. Let us now describe the reconstruction method for this first step:

(1)

We determine the multi-indices
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\neq\emptyset
.

(2)

For each
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
as above,
we determine whether
c_{\underline{0}}
is algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}})
by computing the first minors of maximal order of the corresponding Wilczynski matrix
M_{\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}}^{red}
. Proceeding as in
[
HM19
, Lemma 3.7]
or Lemma
4.6
, it suffices to compute them up to the row indexed by the biggest
\underline{m}\in\mathbb{N}^{\tau}
such that
|\underline{m}|\leq 2d\,d^{\prime}_{\underline{s}}
.

(3)

Let
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
c_{\underline{0}}
is algebraic relatively to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}})
. We reconstruct the
K
-vector space of polynomials corresponding to Equation (
43
) according to the method in Section
4.1
, in particular Lemma
4.4
, applied to
(\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}},\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}})
and
c_{\underline{0}}
. We denote by
{E}_{\underline{l}}
this space.

(4)

For each
{\underline{l}}^{\prime}<_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{l}}
, we set
a_{\underline{k},{\underline{l}}^{\prime},j}:=0
for
(\underline{k},{\underline{l}}^{\prime},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}
.

5.1.2.
Second step

With the notations of the previous section, let
\underline{l}
be such that
E_{\underline{l}}\neq\{0\}
. Let us consider the instances of (
42
) corresponding to the
{\underline{l}}^{\prime}
such that:

(44)

{\underline{l}}<_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{l}}^{\prime}<_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{l}}+(0,\ldots,0,1),

For such
{\underline{l}}^{\prime}
, we claim that the set of indices
\underline{i}
such that
\underline{i}<{\underline{l}}^{\prime}
and
{\underline{i}}\geq_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{l}}
is empty. Indeed, by (
44
), note that
|\underline{l}^{\prime}|=|\underline{l}|
. For such
\underline{i}
, one necessarily has
|\underline{i}|<|\underline{l}^{\prime}|=|\underline{l}|
, but also
|\underline{i}|\geq|\underline{l}|
: a contradiction.

According to (4) at the end of First Step above and to the previous claim, the right hand sides of such instances are equal to 0. Hence, they also are of the same form as (
43
):

(45)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{l}^{\prime},j}(\underline{s}){C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}^{\prime}}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}^{\prime}}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}^{\prime},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}=0\,.

We perform the same method of reconstruction as in the First Step
5.1.1
to determine
E_{\underline{l}^{\prime}}
the
K
-vector space of polynomials corresponding to this equation. Note that
E_{\underline{l}^{\prime}}
might be equal to
\{0\}
.

At this step, for each
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
E_{\underline{l}}\neq\{0\}
from the First Step, we have built the vector spaces
E_{\underline{l}^{\prime}}
(possibly
\{0\}
) of all the coefficients
a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}^{\prime},j}
for
(\underline{k},{\underline{l}}^{\prime},j)\in\mathcal{F}\cup\mathcal{G}
satisfying the instances of (
42
) for

{\underline{l}}^{\prime}<_{\mathrm{grlex}}{\underline{l}}+(0,\ldots,0,1)
.

5.1.3.
Third step

Let
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
E_{\underline{l}}\neq\{0\}
as in the First Step
5.1.1
. We consider the instance of (
42
) corresponding to
{\underline{l}}+(0,\ldots,0,1)
. Note that for
\underline{i}<\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)
, we have that
\underline{i}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\underline{l}
. Applying (4) from the end of the First Step, we obtain:

(46)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0,..,d}a_{{\underline{l}}+(0,\ldots,0,1),j}(\underline{s}){C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}=-\displaystyle\sum_{j=0,..,d}a_{\underline{l},j}(\underline{s})C_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}^{(j)}\,.

Noticing that

C_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}^{(j)}=j\,{C_{\underline{0}}}^{j-1}C_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}
, we get:

(47)

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1),j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}{C_{\underline{0}}}^{j}=-\left(\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}j\,{C_{\underline{0}}}^{j-1}\right)C_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}\,.

There is
\underline{l}\leq_{\textrm{grlex}}\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}
such that
c_{\underline{0}}
and
c_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}
satisfy the latter relation, and
c_{\underline{0}}
satisfies the relations (
43
) and (
45
).

If
c_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}=0
, then there are two cases. Either
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}=\emptyset
i.e. there is no coefficient
a_{\underline{k},\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1),j}
to reconstruct. Or else, we obtain an equation like (
43
) and we derive
E_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}
as in the first and second step.

If
c_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}\neq 0
, let us denote
\theta_{\underline{s},(0,\ldots,0,1)}:=\left(|\hat{\underline{l}}_{0}|+d\right)a+b
where
a
and
b
are as in Lemma
2.9
. By this lemma, there are non-trivial polynomial relations
P_{0}(\underline{s},z_{0})=0
and
P_{1}(\underline{s},z_{0},z_{1})=0
satisfied by
c_{\underline{0}}
and
c_{(0,\ldots,0,1)}
with
\deg_{\underline{s}}P_{j}\leq\theta_{\underline{s},(0,\ldots,0,1)}
,
\deg_{z_{0}}P_{j}\leq d
and
\deg_{z_{1}}P_{1}\leq d
. There are several cases.

\bullet
Suppose that
\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}+(0,\ldots,0,1)}=\emptyset
. Equation (
47
) reduces to:

(48)

\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}\cup\mathcal{G}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}j\,{c_{\underline{0}}}^{j-1}=\displaystyle\sum_{(\underline{k},j)\in\mathcal{F}^{\prime}_{\underline{l}}}a_{\underline{k},\underline{l},j}\underline{s}^{\underline{k}}j\,{c_{\underline{0}}}^{j-1}=0,

which means that
c_{\underline{0}}
is at least a double root of (
43
). We resume the notations of Section
4.1
. Let us denote by
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}_{\underline{l}}
the family corresponding to
\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}
for (
43
), and
\lambda_{\underline{l},\,\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}
the coefficients corresponding to
\lambda_{\,\underline{k},j}^{\underline{k}_{0},j_{0}}
. Formula (
27
) of Lemma
4.4
becomes:
